18:T3d0d2,<p>Pluck a guitar string and the bump you made does not sit still — it splits into two half-height bumps that travel in opposite directions. The string's shape at any instant is the <em>sum</em> of a left-traveller and a right-traveller, each rigid, each gliding at the wave speed <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span>. Nothing about the shapes themselves is fixed yet; the boundary conditions (open vs closed) come later and decide how the two travellers are related. This note establishes only that the split exists and what the parameterization constraints demand of each half. The one non-obvious ingredient is that the wave operator <strong>factorizes</strong> into a left-derivative times a right-derivative; once it does, the general solution is immediate.</p>

<h2 id="dalembert-why-any-function-of-ctpmsigma-solves-the-wave-equation">d'Alembert: why any function of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>±</mo><mi>σ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">ct\pm\sigma</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6984em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">±</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span></span></span></span> solves the wave equation</h2>
<p>The wave equation from <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-ii-classical-strings/11-classical-open-and-closed-motion/wave-equation-and-constraints/" class="wiki-link">Wave equation and constraints</a> is

played on <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi><mo <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mrow><mi The second-order operator has <strong>factorized</strong> into two first-order pieces, and that is what makes the equation trivial to solve. If <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mo>−</mo></msub><mo The general solution is therefore a piece that ignores <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>u</mi><mo>−</mo></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_-</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6389em;vertical-align:-0.2083em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">u</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2583em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">−</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2083em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> plus a piece that ignores <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>u</mi><mo>+</mo></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_+</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6389em;vertical-align:-0.2083em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">u</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2583em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">+</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2083em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>:
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000">\vec X(t,\sigma)=\tfrac12\big(\vec F(u_+)+\vec G(u_-)\big)=\tfrac12\big(\vec F(ct+\sigma)+\vec G(ct-\sigma)\big). \tag{1}</span>

<h2 id="which-is-left--and-which-is-right-moving">Which is left- and which is right-moving</h2>
<p>A "waveform moving at speed <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span>" means the same value of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec F</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5
3.333 2.667 6.667 9 10 19 6.667 24.667 20.333 43.667 41 57 7.333 4.667 11
10.667 11 18 0 6-1 10-3 12s-6.667 5-14 9c-28.667 14.667-53.667 35.667-75 63
-1.333 1.333-3.167 3.5-5.5 6.5s-4 4.833-5 5.5c-1 .667-2.5 1.333-4.5 2s-4.333 1
-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> reappears wherever its argument is held constant. Follow a point of constant argument <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>σ</mi><mo>=</mo><mtext>const</mtext></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">ct+\sigma=\text{const}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6984em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6151em;"></span><span class="mord text"><span class="mord">const</span></span></span></span></span>:
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mi>c</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{d\sigma}{dt}=-c,</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2251em;vertical-align:-0.345em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8801em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">d</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.23em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="frac-line" style="border-bottom-width:0.04em;"></span></span><span style="top:-3.394em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">d</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.345em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7778em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mpunct">,</span></span></span></span>
so the whole pattern <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>σ</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec F(ct+\sigma)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2163em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

<p>Both travel at speed <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> in the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>σ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sigma</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span></span></span></span> coordinate, exactly as a relativistic string demands.</p>
<h2 id="what-the-constraints-say-about-each-mover">What the constraints say about each mover</h2>
<p>The wave equation alone knows nothing of the energy parameterization; that information lives in the two constraints, not yet imposed. class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>; squaring will erase it.) Adding and subtracting isolates each mover cleanly:
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000">\vec X'+\frac1c\dot{\vec X}=\vec F'(u_+),\qquad \vec X'-\frac1c\dot{\vec X}=-\vec G'(u_-). \tag{2}</span>
The two parameterization constraints, added and subtracted, package into the single statement (derived in <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-ii-classical-strings/11-classical-open-and-closed-motion/wave-equation-and-constraints/" class="wiki-link">Wave equation and constraints</a>)
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mrow><mo fence="true">(</mo><msup><mover accent="true"><mi>X</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msup><mo>±</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mover accent="true"><mover accent="true"><mi>X</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo>˙</mo></mover><mo fence="true">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1.</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\left(\vec X'\pm\frac1c\dot{\vec X}\right)^2 = 1.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:2.0572em;vertical-align:-0.65em;"></span><span class="minner"><span class="minner"><span class="mopen delimcenter" style="top:0em;"><span class="delimsizing size2">(</span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

Substituting (2) — the lone minus sign vanishes on squaring — gives one condition per mover:
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000">\boxed{\;|\vec F'(u_+)|^2 = 1,\qquad |\vec G'(u_-)|^2 = 1.\;} \tag{3}</span>
The constraints <strong>decouple</strong>: each waveform is traced at unit speed in its own argument, and the two are otherwise blind to each other. Geometrically, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">u</span></span></span></span> is an <strong>arc-length parameter</strong> along the space curve <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec F(u)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2163em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

The energy parameterization has become pure geometry: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>±</mo><mi>σ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u=ct\pm\sigma</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">u</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6984em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">±</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span></span></span></span> is built from the energy coordinate <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>σ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sigma</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span></span></span></span>, and equal steps of it advance equal lengths along the mover curve.</p>
<p>Be careful about what is and is not fixed here. It is the <em>derivative</em> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec F'</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7519em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">′</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> that is a unit vector, never <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec F</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

The curve <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec F(u)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2163em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">u</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> may wander anywhere in space, loop, or double back; only the <em>speed</em> at which it is traced is pinned to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>. All the physics of a specific string lives in the shapes of these two unit-speed curves.</p>
<h2 id="where-this-goes-next">Where this goes next</h2>
<p>The boundary conditions now break the symmetry between the two movers:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Open string</strong> — the free (Neumann) ends act as mirrors, reflecting the right-mover into the left-mover, so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>G</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec G</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5
3.333 2.667 6.667 9 10 19 6.667 24.667 20.333 43.667 41 57 7.333 4.667 11
10.667 11 18 0 6-1 10-3 12s-6.667 5-14 9c-28.667 14.667-53.667 35.667-75 63
-1.333 1.333-3.167 3.5-5.5 6.5s-4 4.833-5 5.5c-1 .667-2.5 1.333-4.5 2s-4.333 1
-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is tied to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec F</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5
3.333 2.667 6.667 9 10 19 6.667 24.667 20.333 43.667 41 57 7.333 4.667 11
10.667 11 18 0 6-1 10-3 12s-6.667 5-14 9c-28.667 14.667-53.667 35.667-75 63
-1.333 1.333-3.167 3.5-5.5 6.5s-4 4.833-5 5.5c-1 .667-2.5 1.333-4.5 2s-4.333 1
-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and there is really <em>one</em> independent waveform (<a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-ii-classical-strings/11-classical-open-and-closed-motion/open-string-reflection-at-endpoints/" class="wiki-link">Open-string reflection at endpoints</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Closed string</strong> — no ends, no mirrors; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec F</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5
3.333 2.667 6.667 9 10 19 6.667 24.667 20.333 43.667 41 57 7.333 4.667 11
10.667 11 18 0 6-1 10-3 12s-6.667 5-14 9c-28.667 14.667-53.667 35.667-75 63
-1.333 1.333-3.167 3.5-5.5 6.5s-4 4.833-5 5.5c-1 .667-2.5 1.333-4.5 2s-4.333 1
-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>G</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec G</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5
3.333 2.667 6.667 9 10 19 6.667 24.667 20.333 43.667 41 57 7.333 4.667 11
10.667 11 18 0 6-1 10-3 12s-6.667 5-14 9c-28.667 14.667-53.667 35.667-75 63
-1.333 1.333-3.167 3.5-5.5 6.5s-4 4.833-5 5.5c-1 .667-2.5 1.333-4.5 2s-4.333 1
-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> stay <strong>independent</strong>, the classical seed of the closed string's two separate towers of oscillators (<a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-ii-classical-strings/11-classical-open-and-closed-motion/closed-string-periodicity-and-rotating-strings/" class="wiki-link">Closed-string periodicity and rotating strings</a>).</li>
</ul>
<p>That split — one waveform versus two — is the entire difference between open and closed classical motion.</p>
<h2 id="open-questions">Open questions</h2>
<ul>
<li>Closed strings keep independent left/right movers; the quantum consequence (their oscillator levels must still match) is <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-iii-quantization/15-quantum-closed-strings/level-matching/" class="wiki-link">Level matching</a>.</li>
<li>Turning these arbitrary unit-speed curves into a Fourier/oscillator expansion (with the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msqrt><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>α</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msup></mrow></msqrt></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sqrt{2\alpha'}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.04em;vertical-align:-0.1161em;"></span><span class="mord sqrt"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9239em;"><span class="svg-align" style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord" style="padding-left:0.833em;"><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0037em;">α</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6779em;"><span style="top:-2.989em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">′</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span style="top:-2.8839em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="hide-tail" style="min-width:0.853em;height:1.08em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="400em" height="1.08em" viewBox="0 0 400000 1080" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin slice"><path d="M95,702

<h2 id="exercise">Exercise</h2>

<p>The lesson: the constraints are not optional dressing on the wave equation — they <em>require both movers to be present</em>.
